=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
The manufacturing of a certain product requires N processes numbered 1,2,\dots,N.
For each process i, there are two types of machines S_i and T_i available for purchase to handle it.

- Machine S_i: Can process A_i products per day per unit, and costs P_i yen per unit.
- Machine T_i: Can process B_i products per day per unit, and costs Q_i yen per unit.

You can purchase any number of each machine, possibly zero.
Suppose that process i can handle W_i products per day as a result of introducing machines.
Here, we define the production capacity as the minimum of W, that is, \displaystyle \min^{N}_{i=1} W_i.
Given a total budget of X yen, find the maximum achievable production capacity.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N X
A_1 P_1 B_1 Q_1
A_2 P_2 B_2 Q_2
\vdots
A_N P_N B_N Q_N

Output

Print the answer as an integer.

Constraints


- All input values are integers.
- 1 \le N \le 100
- 1 \le A_i,B_i \le 100
- 1 \le P_i,Q_i,X \le 10^7

Sample Input 1

3 22
2 5 3 6
1 1 3 3
1 3 2 4

Sample Output 1

4

For example, by introducing machines as follows, we can achieve a production capacity of 4, which is the maximum possible.

- For process 1, introduce 2 units of machine S_1.
- This allows processing 4 products per day and costs a total of 10 yen.


- For process 2, introduce 1 unit of machine S_2.
- This allows processing 1 product per day and costs a total of 1 yen.


- For process 2, introduce 1 unit of machine T_2.
- This allows processing 3 products per day and costs a total of 3 yen.


- For process 3, introduce 2 units of machine T_3.
- This allows processing 4 products per day and costs a total of 8 yen.

Sample Input 2

1 10000000
100 1 100 1

Sample Output 2

1000000000

Sample Input 3

1 1
1 10000000 1 10000000

Sample Output 3

0

There may be cases where a positive production capacity cannot be achieved.

Sample Input 4

10 7654321
8 6 9 1
5 6 4 3
2 4 7 9
7 8 9 1
7 9 1 6
4 8 9 1
2 2 8 9
1 6 2 6
4 2 3 4
6 6 5 2

Sample Output 4

894742

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

def min_cost(i, cap):
    A,P,B,Q = machines[i]
    if B*P >= A*Q:
        best = INF
        for x in range(B):  # 0..B-1
            rem = cap - A*x
            if rem <=0: y=0
            else: y=(rem + B -1)//B
            cost = x*P + y*Q
            if cost < best: best=cost
        return best
    else:
        best=INF
        for y in range(A):
            rem = cap - B*y
            if rem <=0: x=0
            else: x=(rem + A -1)//A
            cost = x*P + y*Q
            if cost < best: best=cost
        return best

Implementation:
import sys
def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N, X = data[0], data[1]
    machines = []
    idx = 2
    max_ab = 0
    for _ in range(N):
        A = data[idx]; P = data[idx+1]; B = data[idx+2]; Q = data[idx+3]; idx += 4
        machines.append((A,P,B,Q))
        if A > max_ab: max_ab = A
        if B > max_ab: max_ab = B
    INF = 10**30
    def min_cost(cap, m):
        A,P,B,Q = m
        if B * P >= A * Q:
            best = INF
            # enumerate number of S machines
            for x in range(B):
                rem = cap - A * x
                if rem <= 0:
                    y = 0
                else:
                    y = (rem + B - 1) // B
                cost = x * P + y * Q
                if cost < best:
                    best = cost
            return best
        else:
            best = INF
            # enumerate number of T machines
            for y in range(A):
                rem = cap - B * y
                if rem <= 0:
                    x = 0
                else:
                    x = (rem + A - 1) // A
                cost = x * P + y * Q
                if cost < best:
                    best = cost
            return best

    def feasible(cap):
        total = 0
        for m in machines:
            total += min_cost(cap, m)
            if total > X:
                return False
        return total <= X

    lo = 0
    hi = 100 * X + 1
    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if feasible(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    print(lo)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "min_cost" if condition true and B=100, A=1, P=1, Q=100, cap=10099, x=99,y=100 cost10099. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "min_cost" if condition true and B=100, A=1, P=1,
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, X = data[0], data[1]

    machines = []
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        A = data[idx]
        P = data[idx + 1]
        B = data[idx + 2]
        Q = data[idx + 3]
        idx += 4
        machines.append((A, P, B, Q))

    INF = 10**30

    def min_cost(cap, m):
        A, P, B, Q = m

        # If T is cheaper/equal per capacity, it is enough to try x < B.
        if B * P >= A * Q:
            best = INF
            for x in range(B):
                rem = cap - A * x
                if rem <= 0:
                    y = 0
                else:
                    y = (rem + B - 1) // B
                cost = x * P + y * Q
                if cost < best:
                    best = cost
            return best

        # Otherwise S is cheaper/equal per capacity, so try y < A.
        else:
            best = INF
            for y in range(A):
                rem = cap - B * y
                if rem <= 0:
                    x = 0
                else:
                    x = (rem + A - 1) // A
                cost = x * P + y * Q
                if cost < best:
                    best = cost
            return best

    def feasible(cap):
        total = 0
        for m in machines:
            total += min_cost(cap, m)
            if total > X:
                return False
        return True

    # Capacity of any process is at most 100 * money spent on it.
    lo = 0
    hi = 100 * X + 1  # infeasible upper bound

    while hi - lo > 1:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if feasible(mid):
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    print(lo)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
